ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u2	Thank you so much for saying a nice hello to everyone. The new law on legal protection in procurement procedures has ordered the State Audit Commission to take priority decisions on disputes arising from procurement procedures co-financed by the European Union. On this basis, the State Audit Commission also prepared a supplement to the Rules of Procedure, discussed it at its general meeting, adopted it there and then submitted it to the National Assembly for approval. We have also adopted the necessary internal acts to try to adapt our actions to the objectives pursued by the novels. Then, of course, there are human resources and financial constraints in which the State Audit Commission operates, but we have already informed the Committee and the competent ministry, so I will not repeat these arguments here. But I'd like to point out the following. The State Audit Commission is aware of the importance of public procurement for the smooth functioning of the State and local communities and is trying to ensure that the audit disputes are resolved as soon as possible. The PSC provides that the State Audit Commission should decide on the audit procedures within 15 working days, in exceptional cases, where challenging disputes are required when the appointment of an expert, expert and the like is necessary, that period may be extended by 15 working days. So there is a common deadline within which the State Audit Commission can decide on the dispute for 30 working days. Statistics show that the decision-making time for this year is on average about 14 working days per case. This deadline is substantially shorter than the time limits in which any body abroad is actually acting. The Ministry of Finance, which has prepared an analysis of the proposals of the novel ZPVPJN, has only found that, say, such disputes in Germany take place at the first stage of up to 7 weeks, then judicial protection is possible. In Hungary at the first stage up to 90 days, again with the possibility of judicial protection, in the Czech Republic 98 days at the first stage, 94 days at the second stage, in Latvia up to four years at the second stage and so on. In short, I argue that legal protection in audit procedures, as is the case with the State Audit Commission, is provided under the current regime within the shortest possible time limits in which it is even possible to ensure substantive treatment of the matter. However, it should be pointed out that the disputes arising from the procurement procedures are a matter of contentious disputes, whose assessment requires knowledge of domestic and European legislation – the practice of the judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union – and that these are decisions which must withstand not only the judgment of the parties involved in this procedure but later possible redress. You know that 13 disputes have been raised against or related to the decisions of the State Audit Commission, including disputes before the European Commission. The fact that we are doing well shows that none of these conflicts have been successful so far. In short, all the judgments issued concerning the work of the State Audit Commission were rejected. The next thing I would like to point out is because recently, of course, the issue of the effective use of European funds and the problems encountered in this regard has been mentioned for a reason. Only a few data relating to legal protection in procurement procedures. According to the Ministry of Finance, 34 000 or 34 000 public contracts were made in 2012. Of these, 5,000 were published on the public procurement portal or in the Official Journal of the European Union, meaning more medium or larger contracts. During the same period, around 500 disputes were raised before the State Audit Commission. This means that about half of all public contracts awarded in the Republic of Slovenia have been brought into conflict, or else 98.5% have been non-problemsome from this point of view. However, with regard to public contracts co-financed by the European Union, such contracts were cases 61 and about 9 per cent of the public contracts co-financed by EU funds, there was a dispute brought before the State Audit Commission. Here's another piece of information. In 2012, the State Audit Commission granted the application by cancelling the part, or the whole tender documentation or the whole procedure, or the decision to award the contract in 162 cases. This means in less than a third of the requirements that it has addressed, again, even if we are only looking at procurement procedures that were co-financing from EU funds, this percentage is the same. Such orders, where irregularities were found, were less than a third. This means that the impact of the decision-making of the State Audit Commission, or even the legal protection system we have, was such that it reflected in four tenths of one percent of all public contracts awarded in the Republic of Slovenia in 2012 or in less than 3 percent of those contracts co-financed by the European Union. This shows that the issue of efficient European resource utilisation is much more complex than it has been seen in recent times and that the contribution of legal protection to the effectiveness of European resource absorption is very small. The fact is that the State Audit Commission repeals the procurement procedure when it detects an irregular, illegal conduct. The fact is also that the existence of illegal practices is identified by other bodies, the Office for Budgetary Control, including the European Court of Justice, which finds that 20 per cent [[…]] [[sign for the end of the debate]] as a result of serious infringements in public procurement, as well as errors arising out of public procurement, which also stems from the Government's action plan for efficient pumping, such as in the preparation of projects. [[…]] Just a final thought. We are aware of the importance of effective public procurement over the importance of effective use of European funds, and for this reason we also support the initiative presented by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy to include this topic in one of the next meetings, because we certainly support any serious effort towards improving the current situation. Thank you very much.

The President of the State Audit stressed that, in this July, the Law on Legal Protection in Public Procurement Procedures was upgraded by Article 37a, which provides that requests for revision, which identify infringements in procurement procedures co-financed by European funds, are to be decided prioritised by the State Audit Board. At the same time, the audit committee must also harmonise its rules of procedure within three months of the implementation of the Law on Legal Protection. After a short discussion, the Committee adopted a decision to propose to the National Assembly that it should adopt a decision supplementing the Rules of Procedure of the State Audit Commission to revise the procedures for the award of public contracts. ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u9	Thank you. Mr Marko Pavlisič will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u10	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Dear colleagues, dear colleagues! The most recent amendment to the Law on Legal Protection in the procurement procedures that we have dealt with in the National Assembly in July this year following an emergency procedure has provided for a new Article 37a to be adopted in the legal matter, which provides that the audit requests for infringements in the procurement procedures co-financed by European funds shall give priority to the decision of the State Audit Commission. A provision has also been adopted to bring the Rules of Procedure of the State Audit Commission into line with the provisions of this amendment within a period of three months. Content of the Supplement to the Rules of Procedure of the State Audit Commission for the revision of the procedures for the award of public contracts is followed by the above mentioned legal novelties. A new paragraph 22a is added to the Rules of Procedure setting out the order in which matters are dealt with. In accordance with that provision of the Rules of Procedure, the State Audit Commission will give priority to matters for which the law so provides. The other matters will be dealt with as a rule in the order in which they come. Today's revision of the Rules of Procedure changes the functioning of the State Audit Commission in the revision of procurement procedures. We must be aware that the purpose of legal protection in these proceedings is to ensure that all tenderers have the opportunity to obtain a public contract at their source under the same conditions. We must also be aware of the increase in the case of the State Audit Commission, and it is therefore necessary to set out the priorities that this Commission will give priority to. Since public procurement co-financed by European funds is a rule for contracts of higher value, with agreed drawing dynamics, it is essential that infringements are detected in the process of selecting suppliers or operators as quickly as possible, thus helping to ensure that public procurement is swift and effective, and thus making it easier and more efficient to draw on the resources of the European Union. We could say that this smaller "pretty supplement" in the Rules of Procedure is not only the result of coordination with the new Law on Legal Protection in procurement procedures, but even more so indicates steps towards major surgical interventions that will need to be taken in the field of public procurement in order to achieve faster and more effective public procurement, and consequently more efficient absorption of funds from the European Union. As we are aware of the importance of drawing on European funds, as well as of our support for faster and more effective public procurement, we will in the Parliamentary Group of the Civil Code agree to complement the Rules of Procedure. Thank you.

We will therefore today support a similar attempt in Slovenia to implement in the most effective way what they know in the United States as the so-called Chapter 11 as the preventive restructuring process, where the key initiative is that of the indebted debtor of the financial institution, namely the banks, and in the hands of the scissors and the canvas, to take control of the debtor by means of the restructuring process and thus to repay it. We trust the Social Democrats with the arguments of both the Government and the Bank of Slovenia and some international institutions that this will be an effective way of restructuring for the most acute cases. Social Democrats believe that there are not even many constitutional dilemmas on this issue. We believe that capital, which is at zero or even capital under this point, is already negative, in fact economically, not necessarily legally, no longer has the legitimacy of ownership and that at that time the economic owners are already its creditors. Social Democrats hope that this novel will also be recognised through effective legal restructuring procedures. However, we must admit that, because of the complexity of the entire insolvency law, it is legitimate to expect that work on this matter at the normative level should not be stopped, but that the Ministry of Justice, together with the professional institutions, insists on this work and, as soon as possible, introduces a comprehensive new law that will simplify the complex procedures, introduces a greater initiative in the proceedings on the part of the creditors and thus allows for overindebtedness as a prerequisite for recovery. According to our behaviour, the novel on this envisaged restructuring process introduces a somewhat balanced and reasonable agreement on the interests of the debtors and their creditors. In this way, creditors will be able to take over the management of the debtor and the firm will need to agree with the creditors in a forced settlement with unrealistic financial restructuring plans. The conditions we are in today are very uncertain. The problem of over-indebtedness is already being addressed by many domestic companies, who are trying to eliminate insolvency due to difficulties and thus forcible settlement or bankruptcy due to problems with financial creditors, that is, banks. In doing so, suppliers are in a poor position due to unsecured claims. And it is precisely the introduction of the pre-insolvency procedure that allows the company to acquire in time a financial restructuring agreement with banks, i.e. an agreement on the reprogramming of loans. The banks are thus prepared to provide new funds in this process and, in the event of subsequent forced settlement or bankruptcy, the first payment would also be made. The solutions proposed in the enforcement procedure are also appropriate because they also apply to undertakings already in those procedures. All together, it points to a carefully coordinated and planned repurchase of loans or a so-called financial restructuring plan that requires a time limit on compliance with agreed commitments. We all know that when the cash flow falls for companies, the costs of financing the loans leased from the past are rising, often unsecured creditors, suppliers take the shortest. That is why it is still more important for the debtor to remain a functioning company than to end up in bankruptcy. And it is precisely after this change in legislation that you could agree to change the restructuring plan, which is only allowed for medium and large companies. Moreover, the consent of creditors should be reached for more than half of all claims. In the event that we have only unsecured creditors in the event of a forced settlement, the change in the plan should be agreed by the secured creditors. So any change to the plan requires more than half of the creditors to agree. In the DeSUS Group, we believe that both the economic and social picture of Slovenia will improve at least in the sense that such proposed financial restructuring tools will result in economic growth and jobs being revived. We certainly welcome the well-prepared solutions of all those involved, including at the international European level, by which we, of course, expect the earliest implementation in practice. However, another important novelty introduced in the amendment of the law is the abolition of the minimum amount of creditors' payments and the maximum time limit in the enforcement procedures. In New Slovenia, we do not support this proposed solution in law. In this way, the path to abuse this institution is being reopened in a way that will delay the process of forced settlement in a way that will result in damage to the creditors of insolvency companies. Members of the New Slovenia Group will not support the proposed law.

The high indebtedness of companies is also seen as a high exposure to the banking system. So we are in a spiral, because banks on the one hand cannot provide additional capital to the economy and the economy cannot recover and get rid of the spasm in which it is located. And the longer this problem is resolved, the more indebtedness is spread to all spheres of the economy. However, logic is simple: more than business failures, it is harder to ensure the stability of public finances, less companies, less tax inflows, less tax inflows, more difficult to provide a social and solidarity country. The fact is that the average ratio between debt and equity is 145 per cent with us, while the European average is 103 per cent. According to the Slovenian Chamber of Commerce, as many as 10,000 companies in Slovenia are critically indebted. Without fast and, in particular, transparent procedures that will allow companies to be restructured both financially, business and ownershipally, we all know where these companies will end; in bankruptcy, which means the end of the business, the end of jobs and the end of the hope for creditors that their claims will be paid. The proposed novelty will allow for effective restructuring of businesses both within the framework of the compulsory settlement and in cases where insolvency has not yet occurred at all, but there is a fear that this will happen within one year. Under insolvency proceedings, the enforcement procedure is the only way to preserve the company's healthy core and maintain existing jobs. The amendment thus introduces the principle of absolute advantage also in the enforcement procedure, and the principle lays down the order, i.e. the advantage of payment of claims. In order to implement this principle, claims shall be classified in classes according to the priority payment entitlement criterion included. At the same time, specific rules on the simplified reduction of core capital are also laid down to ensure consistent implementation of the principle that so far the shareholders of the debtor can only retain such a share in the company as would have been kept in bankruptcy proceedings, i.e. after the payment of all creditors. Most likely, however, the most important novelty is the introduction of a preventive restructuring procedure which will allow for the conclusion of agreements on the financial restructuring of large and medium-sized companies, one year before insolvency. This is the moment that allows creditors to pay higher financial claims on the one hand and the most effective interpretation exercise on the other. With the agreement to be concluded by banks and businesses in this process, it will be possible to maintain existing jobs and maintain the healthy core of the company. In order for these procedures to be effective, it will be imperative, once the law is in force, that all involved take an appropriate approach to the rescue and dispossession of businesses. Given that the proposal has been prepared in cooperation with the widest possible range of participants supporting the proposal, we believe that the proposed solutions will also come to life in practice. We have waited a long time for the novelty ahead of us, as it represents a very important step in relaunching the economy. The novelty enables a rapid and efficient system-wide extension of businesses, thus providing a real opportunity for economic recovery and for preserving existing jobs in the first phase and creating new jobs in the second stage. Of course, the proposed solutions on the other hand are also a key element in the successful resolution of the banking system. In Positive Slovenia, we will therefore support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u72	Thank you for the floor, Madam President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! In 2011, the European Systemic Risk Committee, the ESRB, adopted a recommendation aimed at improving the effectiveness of macroprudential control in the Member States of the European Union. In its recommendation, the ESRB advised the establishment of a national authority to carry out macro-bonnet control and ensure the stability of the financial system. To this end, a proposal for a law on macro-prudential supervision of the financial system has been prepared to enable the establishment of a Committee on Financial Stability. In accordance with the recommendations of the ESRB, this Committee monitored to ensure financial stability with a view to reducing systemic risks and the sustainable contribution of the financial sector to economic growth. In accordance with its responsibilities, the Committee will be able to formulate recommendations or guidelines to be addressed to a particular supervisory authority. This guidance will not interfere with the independence of the supervisory authority, but it will have to justify non-compliance with the guidelines. We welcome the law in the Civil Gazette, but at the same time we wonder if so many new committees and subcommittees should be formed. In our view, it might be more reasonable that, on the example of some European countries, all powers of control over the financial system should be exercised from one place and thus ensure better transparency of the system while at the same time making the use of public funds more efficient. In the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette, we will support the proposal of the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u77	Thank you very much. On behalf of the New Slovenia Group, Mr Jožef Horvat.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u78	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear colleagues, distinguished colleagues, distinguished Mr. Secretary of State! The proposal for the law under consideration constitutes the implementation of the recommendation of the European Systemic Risk Committee of 22 December 2011, the essence of which is that there is an appropriate supervisory authority at national level for effective – I underline effective – macroprudential supervision. The area of supervision of financial system institutions is divided into several supervisors in our legal order: the Bank of Slovenia, which supervises banks, savings banks and credit institutions, the Securities Market Agency, which supervises the capital market, and the Insurance Supervisory Agency, which supervises insurance undertakings. All these institutions shall exercise control only within the framework of their respective competences. Risks that quickly flow over the entire banking system may start to accumulate in the individual financial sector. The macro-prudential financial supervision law is the legal basis for the establishment of the Financial Stability Committee responsible for the supervision of the financial system as a whole. The Committee shall consist of 8 members, consisting of representatives of existing supervisory institutions, the Securities Market Agency and the Insurance Supervisory Agency, the Ministry of Finance and the Bank of Slovenia. The main task of the Committee is to develop macroprudential policies. The Committee will act as a consultative body in the area of risk assessment for financial stability, obtain and analyse data from individual supervisory authorities, give guidance to supervisory authorities and the like. And now the comments of New Slovenia on the text of the law under consideration. In Novi Slovenija we would like to draw attention to the lack of legal provisions on the responsibility of the members of the Committee for their work within the framework of the Committee on Financial Stability. 20. Article 20 of the bill requires members of the committee to act with the care of a good expert, but does not provide for their material and criminal responsibility for their work or dismissal. Although the Committee is a consultative body, in our view, this full responsibility of the members of the Committee for Individual Decisions would ensure effective macroprudential control, as expected by the European Systemic Risk Committee. Despite these shortcomings in New Slovenia, we believe that the proposal represents a small step, but a step towards better and effective control of the financial system as a whole, and we will therefore support the Members of the New Slovenia Group. Thank you very much.

The Commission received an opinion from the Legal and Legislative Service, which examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the legislative and technical aspects and has no comments on it. The representative of the proponents said in the introductory explanation that the draft law follows the constitutional principles of the division of power, under which the independence of the legislative, executive and judicial branches of government and the independence and independence of local self-government was stressed. Both principles require the establishment of an effective brake system and a balance between operators of different functions of authority. Amendments to the law eliminate the interesting incompatibilities of applications in which multiple officials act simultaneously. The next reason for amending the law, in their view, stems from the constitutional mandate of Members, whereby Members should represent the interests of all citizens. Among other things, the question was raised as to whether such final regulation would also constitute a ban on the fifth part of Members, who would be able to send a Member in that country and how far we would go with it. Following the conclusion of the discussion, a decision was proposed on the basis of Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly and also a vote that the proposal for a law amending the Law on Members was not suitable for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u127	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Slovenian Democratic Party Group, Mr Zvonko Lah has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u128	Thank you. Dear Prime Minister, representatives of the Ministry, colleague and colleague! The proposal for a novel Water Act is the result of a review by the Court of Auditors of the performance of the Ministry of the Environment and Space of that time in the implementation of the Water Act in 2009 and 2010, which concluded that entities have been dealt with unevenly in the payment of water rights. The Water Act of 2002 provided that water must be obtained and paid for a specific use of water with a water permit or concession, with the payment obligation being implemented from the date of exercise of the water right. The law also provided for a transitional period of two years in which the debtors who needed water had to apply for water rights. Some of the pledges have submitted their application in due time and with some of them the government has concluded a concession contract, but with some potential pledges it has not. In addition to these categories of debtors, there are also those who used water and have submitted an application for water rights after the expiry of the transitional period or too late, or have begun to use the water later. This means that in many cases existing water users did not conclude concessions and therefore did not have a financial obligation compared to those entities which concluded and paid them. In view of the unequal treatment of the debtor, the Court of Auditors ordered the Minister of Auditors to settle the matter in such a way that the obligation to pay the concession fee would be fixed for the period of actual exploitation of the water source. This law deals with compensation for the use of water by three categories of debtors, namely producers of drinks, thermal baths and those who need water for heating. In addition to the transitional provisions, this amendment regulates the issue of awarding a concession without a call for tender to all existing water users who should obtain a water right on the basis of a concession but were not granted if it fulfils the conditions for obtaining a concession. In order to align the legal situation of entities that used water and paid water rights in the past period with entities that also needed water and whose water rights were not calculated, the so-called compensation for the use of water for the period of actual water use, as of 1 January 2005, will be regulated by law, with the debtors who have paid more concessions in the past than to pay compensation for water use being reimbursed. The novelty also provides for the calculation and payment of that refund, avoiding excessive interference with the existing financial situation of economic operators, allowing payment to a maximum of 30 meals or 30 years. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we hope that this amendment of the law will help to speed up the resolution of applications in the competent ministry in this area, as some have been waiting for a solution for almost a decade. This must no longer happen in the future.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u129	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Group of Social Democrats, Mr Mirko Brulc has the floor.

The use of water as a basic life and also important strategic goods for natural users in Slovenia is regulated by law differently than for economic use. For a long time, the economic situation in the area of water use has been poorly regulated, and this has led to a situation that must now be addressed by the Proposal for a Law on Supplements to the Water Act. Although quantitative and qualitative water is not lacking in Slovenia, it needs to be handled efficiently and economically, and it also needs to be burdened with adequate financial burden in terms of economic use, as it is ultimately a raw material that is marketed or sold in different forms of the economy. To this end, the Water Act 2002 has laid down the allocation of water licences and anti-payment concessions and the obligation to pay would arise from the date of the exercise of water rights. The law provided for a two-year transitional period during which everyone should apply for a water licence or concession. However, not all users have done so during the prescribed period, which, or now, would give them an additional or better opportunity to do so. What really happened after the adoption of the Water Act in 2002? Users with a timely authorisation or concession have, as payers, been in an unequal position with respect to those users who have not done so. This was also pointed out by the Court of Auditors, which pointed out delays and non-equity and selective treatment in the award of concessions, and consequently put the law's followers in an uncompetitive position, as their water as the primary raw material of their activities became more expensive than those who did not respect the law. In its observations, however, the Court of Auditors, as a corrective measure, also ordered the preparation of such legislation as to provide for the payment of water for the return of those users who have not yet been granted water rights. Perhaps such a corrective measure could also be contrary to Article 155 of the Constitution, which deals with retroactivity, and the Ministry was also aware of that and, according to their assurances, reached an agreement with all stakeholders concerned with the amendment of the law. It is also a fact that water users knew that they had to pay for the use of water and that it should not be used before using a water permit or concession; hence, this fact speaks for itself. Now, I would not say more about what is still the law; for example, it also brings more precise forms of water charging for economic purposes, but I would end up saying that we will support the bill in the National Gazette because it regulates and corrects inequalities in the market. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u133	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, Mr Janez Ribič has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u134	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President of the National Assembly. Dear representatives of the Ministry, Members and Members of Parliament! The Water Act is also one of those laws that repeatedly enters our parliamentary benches. It is essential that this amendment of the law governs one area of operation of the State which, in the last ten years since the new Water Act was in force, has not done its homework; not only has it not done its homework, but it has also lost considerable financial resources in these years because of the unallocated concessions. However, this year's Court of Auditors report has helped to regulate this area more quickly and we now have new articles. What needs to be said here is that all water users will finally be equalized by this regulation and will no longer be in a privileged position to those who do not have a concession contract, although not by their own fault; that water rights procedures can now be completed without a call for tender; that compensation and all that is connected are also regulated; and that these compensations can be paid over a longer period of time so as not to overly affect the operations of these users and, in particular, the jobs. There is certainly also a significant dilemma here when we talk about payback, it is in some way retroactivity, and there is a risk that this law will also be subject to a constitutional judgment. The Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment itself now agrees that the law is not optimal, and the Law-Legal Service and the Court of Auditors have also pointed out some inconsistencies, such as the issue of overpayment credits. However, the Chamber of Commerce drew attention to the question of interest as the remuneration will be paid back over a long period of time. We have heard some other warnings from the Court of Auditors, let us hope that the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment has also heard them and that they will be taken into account in the further changes to the Water Act. For the time being, in the Slovenian People's Party, we believe that the essential purpose of the amendment of the Water Act, which is linked to this year's findings by the Court of Auditors, has been achieved. In order to finally settle matters back and forth, we support this law in the Slovenian People's Party. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u135	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners, Mrs Marija Plevčak has the floor. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u136	Thank you for your word and a warm welcome to everyone! In the DeSUS Group, we are pleased that the Minister of Agriculture has consulted representatives of all affected categories of debtors when preparing the draft Law on Water Change and Supplementation and reached agreement on the amount of compensation for water use, on the possibility of payment by hand and on the calculation of the difference of payments. We hope that other ministers will follow his practice as often as possible, as this avoids many complications and unnecessary perceptions from the interested professional public. The proposed amendment to the Water Act is mainly the result of the Court of Auditors' audit report on the performance of the Ministry of Environment and Space in the implementation of the Water Act in 2009 and 2010. The Ministry found that operators such as drink producers, thermal baths and those who need water for heating are not treated equally in the payment of water rights. In 2002 the Water Act was adopted at that time, providing that water rights must be obtained and paid for the specific use of water with a water permit or concession, with the payment obligation being implemented from the date of the exercise of the water right. During the transitional period of two years during which the debtors who needed water had to submit an application for this water right. Some of the pledges have given up their role in time and with many of them the government has concluded a concession contract, but with some potential pledges it has not. In addition to these categories of debtors, there are also those who used water and have submitted an application for water rights after the expiry of the transitional period or have not submitted it at all. Some of the pledges, however, began to use water later. This means that, in many cases, existing water users did not conclude concessions and therefore did not have or have no financial obligations compared to those of the entities that concluded and paid them. In view of the unequal treatment of the debtors, the Court of Auditors ordered the Ministry of Auditors to address this issue, so that the obligation to pay the concession fee is fixed for the period of actual exploitation of the water source. As an example, the production of drinks had an extremely high duty, about EUR 11 per cubic metre of liquor sold. There is also a difference between the individual sectors within the drinks. In the production of juices and mineral waters, the duty is around 2 euros and the largest payers are beer producers. In the case of thermal water, the amount of the payment to the budget is extremely low, with most natural spas and baths that need thermal water not paying the concession fee at all. A similar situation in the area of water use for heating, where the payment of concession fee as well as water reimbursement is extremely low, less than 1% of total inflows to the water fund. Therefore, one of the essential solutions in this proposal is the regime which will allow the completion of the procedures under Article 199 of the Water Act and the outstanding applications following the expiry of the transitional period, all in a manner that will allow all those categories to be awarded concessions without a call for tender and in this respect to the completion of those procedures. In order to realize the payment for water rights during the actual use of water, the novel introduces a new legal standard – compensation for water use as well as the debtors, method of calculation and method of payment of this compensation. However, the second key solution to this proposal is the addition of Article 162 of the Law, which is the result of the budget rebalancing in connection with the provision of smooth financing for the compulsory economic and public water management service. The proposed new provision establishes the Water Fund as a source of funding for this economic public service. In the Parliamentary Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners, we will support the proposal for a law on supplementing the Water Act.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u139	Thank you very much. We are moving on to shifting the positions of the groups of Members. Mr Jože Velinja will be the first to present the position of the Group of Positive Slovenia.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u140	Mr President, Members and Members of Parliament! At the proposal of the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Intellectual Property, the Government forwarded to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia a proposal for a law amending the Industrial Property Act. It is proposed to amend Article 67 of the current law, which provides for a clearer beginning of the subjective period at the Institute for the continuation of the procedure after the delay and introduces an objective deadline for the submission of the request. In the procedure for obtaining rights at the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for intellectual property, the applicant shall be required to delay the time limits for fulfilling the obligations resulting in his application being deemed withdrawn. The proposal therefore introduces two legal institutes, namely, the return to the previous situation and the continuation of the late procedure. Both institutes should only be used in exceptional cases where the party for various reasons could not submit a request on time. The aim of the proposed amendment is to clarify the duration of the subjective period – it will now run two months from the date on which the cause of the delay has ceased, and if the applicant later finds out about the delay, from the date on which he has completed and not more than two months after the notification of the delay and its legal consequences – and to establish an objective deadline at the Institute for the continuation of the procedure after the delay. The application for continuation of the delay procedure will be submitted by the notifier within six months of the date of the delay and no later. The proposed amendment retains the possibility of requiring the continuation of the applicant procedure and establishes legal certainty. This is a less difficult amendment to the current law, which will not affect the fundamental regulation of industrial property rights, but will provide greater legal certainty as to the rights in force, so it has been delayed after a reduced procedure. In the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia, we will support the proposal of the law.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u141	Thank you very much. On behalf of the SDS Group, Mr Franz Breznik has the floor. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u142	Dear Vice President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! Intellectual property rights, including patents, models, brands, copyright and related rights, etc., are extremely important from an economic point of view because they affect the competitiveness and performance of entities on the market itself. The area of intellectual property is governed by international treaties and European regulations and, in essence, three laws in Slovenia, in addition to the Law on Industrial Property, the Law on Copyright and Related Rights and the Law on the Protection of Topography of Semiconductor Bonds. The acquisition of intellectual property rights is an important step towards securing such property, but even more important are the procedures by which these rights are exercised or defended in the event of an infringement. The new law that we have before us speaks or regulates the exact procedures for the enforcement of industrial property. Thus, in this law, the time limit within which the applicant for industrial property rights, patent, model, brand or geographical indication may request that the Office continue the process of obtaining industrial property rights. The subjective period of two months shall remain unchanged, only the beginning of its course shall be changed, so that it shall run from the date on which the cause of the delay has ceased, provided that the notifier has only subsequently found out of the delay and from the day on which it has been informed. Thus, the beginning of the subjective period shall be determined in the same way as at the institute of return to the previous situation. An objective time limit is therefore set for the submission of a request for the continuation of the late procedure, after which the applicant may no longer require the continuation of the late procedure. The applicant will therefore be able to submit the application no later than six months after the date of the delay and no later. However, for the successful continuation of the procedure after the delay and the removal of its legal consequences, without prejudice to the prudence of its conduct, the applicant must submit a request in such a way as to be timely in relation to the subjective and objective period. The establishment of an objective deadline ensures legal certainty as to the validity of industrial property rights, as it is prevented from continuing the procedure several years after a certain period of time in the process of obtaining the right. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the amendment of the Industrial Property Act. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u143	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Group of Social Democrats, Mr Branko Smodiš has the floor. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u144	Thank you for the floor, Madam President. All present, good afternoon! The amendment to the Industrial Property Act, which we are considering today, refers to Article 67 of the Law governing a specific legal institution, which is a requirement for the continuation of the procedure in cases where the applicant is late in fulfilling the obligations relating to the application or registration of a patent, model, brand or geographical indication with the Office of Intellectual Property. The purpose of this institute is that the notifier may request the continuation of the procedure, irrespective of the reason for his delay in fulfilling the obligation, without any penalty for his delay. Such protection is guaranteed to the applicant by the so-called European Patent Convention. The current regulation, however, has shown the weakness of this legal institution, as the deadline for submitting a request for continuation of the procedure is, in other words, unlimited. It is now considered that the applicant shall only be bound by a period of two months, which shall not start to run until the date on which the applicant has been informed of the delay. This may also be years later, as the European Patent Office or the European Patent Applicants does not specifically inform about the late date. Therefore, only subjective limitation period is laid down, as confirmed by the case law of the Administrative and Supreme Court. Such regulation, however, entails legal uncertainty or economic risk for those economic operators who wish to use an invention that is not protected by a patent in the Republic of Slovenia for economic purposes. Due to the amendment of the law that we are considering today, an objective time-limit is added to this legal institution in addition to the subjective time-limit, similar to that of Switzerland. At the same time, the beginning of this is defined slightly differently than it is now. Following the entry into force of this Act, a request for continuation of the procedure will be made within two months of the date of expiry of the delay or within two months of the notice of late but not later than six months after the date of the delay. Such arrangements will enable the IPR to provide real data on the industrial property rights in force in the Republic of Slovenia, which will, on the one hand, increase legal certainty and, on the other hand, reduce the number of disputes, in particular as regards the validity of European patents in our country. For this reason, the Social Democrats' Group will support the bill. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u145	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Group of the National Gazette, Mr Marko Pavlisic, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u146	Thank you for your word. Dear colleagues, dear colleagues! The Institute of Continuation of Late Procedure was implemented into the Slovenian legal system not so long ago. This was precisely in 2001 with the adoption of the Industrial Property Act. The proposer pursued the objective of achieving greater efficiency in the protection of industrial property rights and of simplifying procedures for obtaining those rights. At the same time, with the introduction of this institute, the law has made many other changes that have been necessary to bring our legislation into line with the existing directives and international acts. Slovenia was preparing to enter the European Union at that time. Since there are different legal provisions in national legislation to remedy the consequences of the delay in European patent holders and since European patent holders are not informed of the delay, the current legal formulation of the Institute for the continuation of the procedure after the delay allows holders of the European patent to apply the European patent in the Republic of Slovenia after several months or years or for an unknown period. As has already been said, the inadequacy or time limit of the use of this institute represents legal uncertainty and economic risk for all those economic operators established in the Republic of Slovenia who wish to use their new products for economic purposes. Since they rely on the situation in our patent register at the time of application, they may later suffer serious damage if a particular economic operator, the holder of a European patent, subsequently registers his patent in front of our body over the years. In order to remove this anomaly, the proposer shall follow the Swiss model by means of a proposal and, inter alia, at the Institute for the continuation of the delay procedure, shall introduce an objective period of six months from the date of the delay. At the same time, the draft law provides for a clearer deadline for the submission of a request for the continuation of the late procedure. The latter will be determined in the same way as at the institute of return to the previous situation. Until now, it has been considered that the continuation of the procedure may be lodged within two months of the date on which the applicant was informed of the delay or its legal consequences. After a new one, two months will be counted from the day that the cause of the delay has ceased, if the applicant finds out of the delay only later, and it will be counted from the day that he finds out. In the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette, we are committed to removing administrative obstacles for citizens and businesses and for a more competitive business environment, and we will therefore support this proposal of the law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u147	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Slovenian People's Party Group, Mr Janez Ribič, please.

Following the new amendment, rightholders will be able to request the continuation of the procedure for obtaining the right no later than six months after the delay and no longer within an unlimited period of time. Under the law in force so far, the procedure could have continued for several years after a period of time in the process of obtaining justice. And the new law limits this time to six months. Therefore, after the expiry of this period, businesses will no longer be at risk of potential patent infringements than they have been before. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party we support this addition to the Industrial Property Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u158	Thank you very much. Allow me to present the position of the Social Democrats' Group on the bill. The proposal for a law introduces only one relevant supplement, a condition of 10 members for the registration of a religious community. With number 10, the Social Democrats have no problems, but of course they would be much more pleased if it were enough to register three citizens or residents of the Republic of Slovenia. The bill therefore does not eliminate unconstitutionality, but merely fills the void after the Constitutional Court has been repealed. Registration or other acquisition of a legal personality is not a condition for the establishment and functioning of a religious community. Religious communities can act completely informally and such association enjoys constitutional protection. However, the Constitutional Court has already explained that the constitutionally protected free operation of religious communities obliges the State to establish a mechanism that allows religious communities wishing to do so to gain legal personality status. It is no coincidence, therefore, that representatives of the largest religious communities have often expressed in public the opinion that there is no need to amend, supplement or any other applicable law, because religious communities can operate under the law we adopted in the National Assembly and that we have opposed to it by the Social Democrats when it is adopted. But we have not only seen the number needed for registration, but we have seen the shortcomings. We have also identified some other shortcomings and inconsistencies either with the Constitution or with international legal acts. Therefore, the proposed novel did not meet our expectations because it eliminates only that legal void. We believe that the Law on Religious Freedom should be based on the principle of real separation between the State and the church and should guarantee religious and mental freedom. The principle of ensuring the equal position of all religious and visual communities should be implemented in a consistent manner. Solutions should also reflect the general expectations of adequate and transparent access to public funding. It is precisely because of the above-mentioned Group of Social Democrats that the amendment will not be supported. However, in its decision, the Constitutional Court has only examined those matters which have been challenged or proposed for constitutional review. Thus, the proportion of what is unacceptable to the Social Democrats in the current law relates in particular to the non-inclusion of visual groups broader than what the Constitutional Court has said. The amendment of New Slovenia, which increases the number 10 to 20 or proposes such an increase, is why we did not support it. What we would certainly like to mention is that we are aware that the issue that we are talking about in this law is an important issue for citizens and citizens of Slovenia, but that all the consideration so far of the bill proposals, which were more and more, shows that the consensus between the majority position of the majority religious communities and those who do not belong to these religious communities is a difficult ideal, which makes it difficult for us to believe that in a short time it will be possible to adopt a larger novel that the Ministry of Culture predicts. The fact that this issue is not empty also shows the reactions of the adoption of the Real Estate Tax Act, in which the Commission has also expressed its fear at the Slovenian Conference of Justice and Peace, both of the consequences of the Real Estate Tax Act and of the announced amendment of the Religious Freedom Act. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u159	Thank you very much. The following is a presentation of the position of the Parliamentary Group of the National List, Mrs Polonca Komar, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u160	Thank you very much for your words, Madam President. Government representatives! On 15 April 2010, the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia issued a decision repealing, inter alia, the first paragraph of Article 13 of the Religious Freedom Act with a one-year grace period, in a constitutional procedure for the assessment of constitutionality initiated at the request of the Council of State on 15 April 2010. The repealed provision laid down the following conditions for the registration of a church or other religious community: at least 100 full-year members who are nationals of Slovenia or foreigners who have a permanent residence on its territory and that the religious community has operated in the Republic of Slovenia for at least the last 10 years. According to the position of the Constitutional Court, the legislator may prescribe minimum membership, but he must be aware that a very high threshold already constitutes an intervention in human rights. The requirements laid down in the first paragraph of Article 41 of the Constitution and the right to freedom of association referred to in Article 42 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia have been excessively affected by the exercise of the right to freedom of conscience referred to in Article 41(1). We are aware that the determination of any number of members as a condition for registration, which is less than 100, is arbitrary. In the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette, we believe that the condition for registration provided for by this amendment is at least 10 full-year members, who are citizens of the Republic of Slovenia or foreigners who have a permanent residence in its territory, and that the requirement that the religious community operate in the Republic of Slovenia for at least the last 10 years is proportionate and makes it possible to exercise those human rights, so we will support the proposal for a law. Thank you.

The Accreditation Commission, appointed by the Expert Council, will give the expert board an opinion on the compatibility of the proposal on new higher education curricula with the starting point and issue an agreement on the appointment of lecturers. A re-accreditation process will also be introduced to include the competent quality agency in higher education. Students' rights will also be regulated in relation to the responsibility of students to fulfil their obligations under study programmes and, due to fictitious enrolment in higher education, the rights of higher education students will have to be restricted. This amendment has not been adopted by the members and members of the Committee. With regard to Article 8 of the novel, the representative of the Student Organisation of Slovenia said that their organisation advocated that the professional council was fairly represented by all stakeholders co-forming the higher school space. He also proposed a different composition of the Accreditation Commission and a two-year term of office for members of the Commission who would come from among the students. The representative of the community of higher vocational schools explained that the community of higher vocational schools should propose members to the Accreditation Commission. The representative of the Ministry explained that the Accreditation Commission was not a committee that would present individual stakeholders, but was an expert in higher vocational education. The student organisation of Slovenia can propose an expert in this field, since it is the composition of a commission which is professional. Similar arrangements are also elsewhere within the framework of the Higher Education Act. The debate was also held on other articles of the novel and members of the Committee welcomed its admission in principle. ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u176	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Mr. Secretary, Members and Members of Parliament! The Higher Education Act was adopted in 2004. Its fundamental feature is that it has regulated higher vocational education as a short-term higher education, although it has maintained links with vocational and vocational education at the secondary level. The amendment of the law before us simplifies and combines accreditation procedures by introducing a single validation procedure for the programme and lecturers, which will be linked to the external quality assessment. A re-accreditation process will also be introduced to include the Quality Agency in higher education. The novel also removes some of the shortcomings that have so far appeared in the implementation of the law itself. It is important to regulate the rights of students in relation to their duties. In practice, however, there is a growing number of students who do not intend to study and get a higher vocational education because they are actually studying in higher vocational schools or colleges, and they are enrolled in higher vocational schools only for obtaining student status, which brings them certain benefits. Thus, the future of a regular study, together with so-called fictitious entries, is only around 40%. It is therefore the duty of students to fulfil, in particular, the mandatory components of study programmes such as exercises and practical education. It is also important to note the new article 13 of the novel, which gives priority to candidates who have not been enrolled in regular studies in higher education or higher education in the selection process. The current arrangements whereby schools were selected only on the basis of results or estimates in previous education, while limiting enrolment, made it possible that the most successful candidates were, in particular, those already enrolled in higher education, and that was a different choice for them, most often due to the provision of student status. However, there have been poorer opportunities for candidates who have completed vocational and professional education and would like to upgrade their education in the selected field of expertise in higher education. Therefore, it makes sense that, in the event of a restriction of entry, those candidates who first take part in tertiary education have priority. With the amendment of the Higher Vocational Education Act, we create the conditions for the rapid adaptation of higher vocational education to the needs of the economy, with the aim or desire that theoretical knowledge be used as quickly and as best as possible in practice, so we will support the draft law in the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette. However, there is another law currently in public debate, which is likely to require much more effort and compromise in order to harmonise stakeholders. I am speaking, of course, of the law on higher education, which will soon be before our benches. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u206	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! You are faced with important changes to the labour market regulation law, which relate mainly to labour intervention agencies or agencies. The key objective of the amendments is to limit infringements of workers' rights in this area and, on the other hand, to ensure higher quality of services provided. The proposal for legislative changes was created in cooperation with the unions and the Association of Employment Agencies. He received unanimous support from the Economic and Social Council. The key changes brought by the novelties relate to a new clearer definition of work-providing activities to another user. New conditions have been added for carrying out activities which, for example, the employer will have to have, as its main activity, a registered intervention of the temporary labour force if he wishes to carry out this activity. The employer will also have to provide a bank guarantee of at least 30 000 euros, and a foreign legal or natural person will have to have a branch in Slovenia if he wants to carry out this activity. We are also complementing the employer's obligation to prevent the establishment of a relationship between the employer and the user, all the ownership-related companies. As a prerequisite for entering into registers or registers, which is also a prerequisite for the start of an operation, a new authorisation for the exercise of an activity shall be introduced. There will also be increased surveillance options, and the submission of an independent auditor's report on the operation of such an agency will be introduced, and we will also introduce monitoring of the implementation of activities by a special expert commission appointed by the Minister or Minister responsible for the work. There are new fines for infringements of the statutory provisions where a higher range is established and the fines themselves are higher. I believe that these changes will make more order in our labour market, but we are certainly aware that legal changes are not enough, that it is particularly important to monitor the implementation of these legislative changes and to monitor effectively. I believe that you will support the amendments that have been tabled. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u208	The Committee considered the draft law on amendments and additions to the Labour Market Regulation Act submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption following a reduced procedure and endorsed by the College. Representatives of the proposer with the Minister for Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities participated in the work of the Committee, a representative of the Legislative and Legal Service and representatives of the professional and interested public. The representative of the proposer, the Minister for Labour, the Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities briefly presented the draft law, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service said that the comments were duly taken into account in the amendments of the coalition parliamentary groups. The representative of the Council of State presented the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons, which supports the proposal of the law. In the debate on articles and amendments tabled in Article 2, it was pointed out by the trade union representative that the proposed amendment to Article 85 of the current law would allow for a more active role for the social partners in the labour market. It was pointed out that the proposed provision did not implement the proposed amendment to Article 2. In this regard, the Minister replied that the basic objective is indeed to enable trade unions and employers to play a more active role in the labour market, so the concerns raised will be examined and, if possible, the proposed amendment in the next legislative procedure, which would of course complement the provision. With regard to Article 7, which strengthens the conditions for carrying out the temporary labour intervention activity, support was expressed by all discussers for any solution that would help to prevent abuse and exploitation of workers. With regard to the amendment, which also provides for the submission of a bank guarantee of at least EUR 30 000, the representative of the Employment Agency Association noted the difficulties faced by some agencies that are already trying to obtain bank guarantees to cover transactions with larger subscribers or users. He pointed out that, as a result, this provision was more written on the skin of large international agencies, while it could pose an additional problem for smaller, in particular domestic agencies to carry out their activities. At the end, the Committee adopted amendments to the coalition groups. Prepared for second reading at the National Assembly is the complementary proposal of the law.

It is the implementation of a judicial decision, i.e. something the State is obliged to pay in any case. This annex was concluded a month after the right to this so-called third quarter was created. At that time, the government took a great legal risk, as part of the profession warned that the annex should not be concluded retroactively. In fact, the annex was concluded in November, and on 1 October, the right was already in the west. In March this year, we have submitted an audit as an exceptional legal remedy to determine whether the highest courts in the country were correct in this case and to clarify all legal dilemmas. We need it because it is necessary to pay for all public servants, not just those represented by trade unions who have filed an action or not only for those against whom the Supreme Court so decided. Of course within the current budget, within the current financial plans. We have a budget with an adequate deficit, which the European Commission has also assessed very positively, and here we need to be careful and careful that we stick to it. The payment is one-off, so it is not a structural increase in public expenditure, but a one-off payment of a back-up bill. The law is coordinated in social dialogue with the vast majority of trade unions in the public sector. If the payment were not settled, there would be 100,000 new lawsuits and executions, and all the years would have been delayed interest, the state should have settled all the costs of the proceedings. With regard to late interest, I have already said, of course, I am in favour of paying as soon as possible, but within the framework of the possibility of budgetary users. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u236	Thank you, Mr. President. Minister with the team! The Internal Policy Committee, the Public Administration and the Local Self-Government discussed at its three sessions the proposal for a law on how to pay the pay gap due to the removal of the third quarter of the imbalances in the basic salaries of public servants. The College of the President of the National Assembly decided at its 72nd meeting that the draft law should be considered on an urgent basis and the Committee therefore considered it on the basis of Rule 144 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. Representatives of the proposer with the Minister of the Interior, Dr. Gregor Virant, representative of the Legislative and Legal Service, representative of the State Council, representative of the Union of State Organs of Slovenia Franšek Verk and Drago Ščernjavič, representative of the Confederation of Public Sector Unions Branimir Štrukelj, representative of the Confederation of Trade Unions of Slovenia Pergam Dr. Janez Posedi, representative of the Union of Soldiers of Slovenia Guido Novak and representative of the Union of Police of Slovenia Zoran Petrovič. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the technical legal aspects. The Committee was also informed of the opinion of the State Council Commission on State Regulations, which supported the proposal for a law. Amendments to Articles 2, 3, 4 and 6 were tabled within the time limit and a proposal was proposed for amendments to the amendment of the coalition groups to Articles 2 and 4, which were prepared on the basis of the opinion of the PSC and adopted by the Committee as its amendments. I will not report on the debate of the representative of the proposalr, because it is identical to what happened a few minutes ago. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service said that the bill regulates the method of payment of remuneration differences due to the elimination of the third quarter of the imbalances in basic salaries for the period from 1 October 2010 to 31 May 2012. The basis for the proposed solution is a violation of Article 50 of the collective agreement with the public sector in a legally binding collective labour dispute before the Supreme Court and the decision that the Republic of Slovenia is obliged to recognise and pay a third quarter of the wage imbalances in basic salaries with statutory late earnings flowing from the maturity of the individual salary. The applicant has decided to treat all beneficiaries equally and thus to provide them with a solution which would provide them with payment, regardless of whether their rights have been recognised by a judicial decision and whether or not they are still in a working relationship at the time of payment. For reasons of public finances, it proposes to pay interest in 2014 and 2015. The representative of the Council of State said that the Commission supported the proposal of the law for state regulation, with members of the Commission finding that some local communities, some 60 municipalities, and some institutions had already paid the difference and that the non-admission of the law would further increase the differences over the imbalances paid. Representatives of the Slovenian Armed Forces Union, the Slovenian National Authorities Union and the Slovenian Police Union said they were opposed to the adoption of such a bill as had been drafted by the Government and had warned that, with such a bill, both the executive and the legislative branches of the authorities were interfering with the judiciary, which is contrary to Article 3 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. The representative of the Police Union of Slovenia also pointed out that the proposal for a law interferes directly with the already acquired rights of public servants by a judicial decision. This is why the rights that have fallen on an individual level are at stake, so trade unions are no longer in a position to coordinate with this. However, the representative of the Slovenian Armed Forces Union pointed out that, if adopted, the draft law would be sent to the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia for review. He also briefed the members and members of the Committee on the letter sent by this union to the European Commission. In the debate, some members of the Committee considered that such a proposal would directly interfere with the constitutional provision on the division of power. A procedural proposal has also been proposed by the opposition to bring a constitutional complaint against the Supreme Court and the Supreme Court, which has not been adopted. Therefore, some members and members of the Committee wanted to invite the PSC to draw up an opinion on whether or not the State or on its behalf could file a constitutional complaint. With regard to this legal issue, the Minister pointed out that the Institute of Constitutional Complaints is an instrument for the protection of fundamental human rights and freedoms, protecting citizens and citizens from the state, and therefore the State cannot be the one making a constitutional complaint against itself. In the debate on Articles 3 and 4, some members of the Committee felt that the Ministry should pay the payment of the pay gap to public servants immediately, since otherwise there were still new costs due to late interest. With regard to the debate on Article 6 of the bill, the members and members of the committee discussed the substantive issue of the constitutional complaint, which was raised at the time of the draft decision of the SDS Group of Ambassadors, and therefore [[...]] [[sign for the end of the debate]] The representative stressed that such a proposed solution goes beyond the powers of the National Assembly as legislator and that it falls within the competence of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. [[...]] The Committee adopted amendments to Articles 2, 3 and 4 of the amendments to the amendment to Article 2 of the amendment to the amendment to Article 2. The Committee did not adopt the amendment of Article 6 of the SDS Group. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended draft law is an integral part of this report. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u241	Thank you. Mr Jerko Čehovin will present the position of the Parliamentary Group Positive Slovenia.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u242	Thank you. Dear colleagues and colleagues, this law is not a consequence of the functioning of the government of Alenka Bratušek. The Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia decided that the annex 4h of the Public Sector Collective Contract, which stipulated that the third quarter of the wage imbalances in the basic salaries of public servants would begin to be paid within two consecutive years following the year when GDP growth would exceed 2.5 % and entered into force on 9 November 2010 unlawfully and, contrary to the Constitution, regulated the right to up to three quarters of the imbalances in return, since the right to payment to public servants was already valid as of 1 October 2010 on the basis of annex No 2. In Positive Slovenia, we take the view that the Court's decision must be respected unconditionally, but that Slovenia is currently in such a situation when immediate payment in a single amount of more than 175 million euros is possible in only two ways. Either by providing these funds on the revenue side, i.e. by increasing or introducing any additional tax, or by providing such funds by reducing funds on the expenditure side, i.e. by reducing the salaries of public servants, reducing pensions or by interfering with social transfers, i.e. monetary social assistance and a security allowance. The immediate implementation of the judgment would inevitably result in the urgent adoption of additional austerity measures, which we do not agree to in Positive Slovenia. We are therefore pleased that the current situation is aware of both the negotiating group of representative public sector unions that have signed a declaration of degree of coordination and that the proposal for a law is in line with 26 of the 36 trade unions. The draft law thus provides that payment of the pay gap for the elimination of disproportionates shall be made in a maximum of two instalments, in such a way that the first instalment, together with the late payments, is paid in February 2014 and the second instalment at the latest in January 2015. The law therefore provides for a means of payment for all civil servants, irrespective of the fact that the court has issued a judgment in a specific collective dispute to which this difference has not yet been paid and ensures equal treatment of all those entitled to payment, ensures a transparent and uniform manner of payment, prevents additional costs of judicial proceedings in individual and collective labour disputes arising and prevents a negative impact on judicial backlogs. What is most important to us is that it maintains sustainable public finances without the need for further intervention in the salaries of public servants, pensions or social transfers on the one hand, and without any additional burden on citizens and citizens on the other. As a result, both the cash budgets and the local cash registers will find their reserves. The proposal for a law on how to pay the pay gap due to the removal of the third quarter of the imbalances in the basic salaries of public servants will be supported. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u247	Thank you. Mag. Katarina Hočevar will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the National Gazette.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u248	Thank you for your word. A warm welcome to the Minister with his fellow Members and all the Members present! The Supreme Court confirmed the Supreme Court’s judgment and ruled that the Republic of Slovenia is obliged to pay public servants the difference under the third quarter of the abolition of wage imbalances. All regular and extraordinary legal remedies were also exhausted. And since Slovenia is a rule of law, we must respect the judgments of the courts, both European and our own. The purpose of this law, which is before us today, is to follow the Supreme Court's rulings and to determine how to pay the pay gap due to the abolition of the third quarter of the disproportionates for the period from 1 October 2010 to 31 May 2012, to all public servants whose difference has not yet been paid. The purpose of the law is thus to ensure equal treatment of all public servants who are entitled to it, irrespective of whether their rights have been recognised by a judicial decision and whether they are still in the public sector at the time of payment. The judgment, as we know, has been tabled by trade unions representing public officials in the state administration. All those who are wrapped up every day with new and increasing taxes to the point that the Slovenian economy is dying and that we are the only country next to Cyprus In the European Union forecasting a new fall, a new economic disaster in the coming years. This, ladies and gentlemen, is the truth, and it is therefore necessary to support the amendment proposed by the SDS to make it pay immediately, because this is, despite the fact that it will require another debt, still cheaper than paying late interest. Because it's the most expensive form of credit. And those who have so argued, forgive me, these are not negotiations.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u311	Thank you very much, Your Honor. The second sub-chapter in Chapter 15 reads: "A higher level of regional and regional cultural integration of minorities." And all of you, who respect the Slovenian Constitution and know the Slovenian Constitution, are convinced that it is meant to be a Hungarian and Italian minority, which are constitutional categories in this country. However, it follows from the text that it is ethnic groups, German speaking and Serbian in the White Krajina.
