I have worked very hard on the committee and on the plenary to accept this amendment, but in view of the resistance of some, I think it is a conscious attempt at what I mentioned at the outset. This way through the last door in Slovenia we introduce a German-speaking minority and a Serbian-speaking minority in Slovenia. We know that there are extremely strong interests after such a change in the Slovenian Constitution and such a constitutional category, which is why this needs to be changed in this chapter at this time and in this matter in order to preserve the integrity of the Slovenian Constitution and the Slovenian state. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u313	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. This amendment cannot be supported because the chapter in which the amendment is to be included bears the title of Human Rights and the protection of cultural diversity. In particular, I stress that human rights are at stake and that the right to one's own culture, expression of culture and development of one's own culture is a fundamental human right. If the title were so renamed as proposed here: A higher level of regional and regional cultural integration of ethnic groups, we would miss the point. This is a higher level of regional and regional cultural integration of minorities. The minority expression is used not only in this resolution but also in the resolution on language policy. This is the opposite of a minority: the majority, the minorities are different, and this text must remain so written as to include, as it is said, such an approach also allows analogous use for other minority communities, not only for nomadic Germans, which are why we specifically refer to an interstate agreement. This is also the case for other groups that have either an inter-State agreement and this diversity needs to be specifically integrated into the national cultural programme. It's cultural rights, not political rights. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u315	I am very sorry that I will have to vote against the resolution, although I support and agree with the content of the resolution. However, due to the respect and commitment of the Slovenian Constitution and the respect of the Slovenian people, I am obliged to vote against it because the amendment to Chapter 15 has not been adopted to the second sub-chapter. In this sub-chapter, in my firm belief, an implicit attempt is made, through the back door, to prejudge the existence of two additional minorities with a constitutional category. We know how these procedures are going, and it is entirely irresponsible that, with a well-intentioned warning and a true dictatorship in the text of this chapter, it persists in such an address that absolutely triggers certain implications that can cause very serious consequences. We know that these aspirations for the Serb minority in Slovenia are very strong, and here the doors are opened by such minorities as, of course, German speakers. This is not denial, as I say, of cultural identity and of all the rights of ethnic groups. It is about avoiding consequences and implications and respecting the Slovenian Constitution and the Slovenian people. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-27-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u317	Thank you so much for saying that. We have before us an appointment, a staff appointment as a member of the State Audit Commission. There is no doubt among us that this is an important institution where the correctness of work and the treatment of people with the same criteria is, in fact, a prerequisite for talking about things going on in some appropriate way. Already on the Commission's terms of reference, they have pointed out that, with the proposal chosen on the Commission's mandate, some things do not mean that things will go well on the State Audit Commission as well. We have received a proposal from a candidate who has been forwarded to the Mandate-Election Commission, and in that part concerning the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities has come to the document for manual correction and no one has signed this amendment. It is not known when this correction was made, who made it, and given that the candidate is running for the State Audit Commission, it is unlikely that this does not mean that the accession to the candidacy in this part is completely corrected. If this has occurred in the appointment of a member of the State Audit Commission, then the audit procedures to be dealt with on this committee may be similarly. However, it is a matter of handling complaints on public tenders, and given that we have probably had a lot of bad experience so far with swaps of cassettes, diskette replacements and so on, such a correction, such a mark that is not marked and not signed, probably does not give good promises that work on the State Audit Board will take place in some appropriate and corrective manner. The second thing that is quite clear from this proposal is that the candidate comes from the Positive Slovenia Group. I do not mind that experts, members of the parties are running, but someone who is one year in the training of the National Assembly or more is essentially working for the political group, and then can nominate as a member of the State Audit Commission is probably not a candidate who can be fully trusted. It's probably one of the models again how important state institutions are being recruited according to some political criteria. None of these candidates were eligible, and a candidate who was the only candidate fell on the previous call, and apparently this part is also involved in a policy of political recruitment to these functions. Just as it happened in the Court of Justice, as it does in a number of other important institutions that manage state assets. In this case, however, it will be a matter of reviewing the procurement procedures. I think that this approach, on the one hand, the correction of the application and, on the other hand, political staffing also in cases where political independence, neutrality and, on the other hand, is required, suggests that we can promise ourselves in the State Audit Commission perhaps even more deformed procedures and findings as they have already been carried out. A wide range of audit projects have fallen unjustifiably, huge problems, the loss of large European funds and so on. But now the candidate is coming, and apparently the majority will choose him who doesn't, I will say, even my candidacy or this approach is not without error and is not corrected. We will not support this proposal in our Group of Members.
